Cruces

Opuscula theologica et polemica

Maximus the Confessor — read the work →

These are the edition's working notes, published as they were written. They record, work by work, where Migne's plate is defective or the reading uncertain: the printed form, what the English does with it, and why. They are not a groomed apparatus. Where a note argues with itself, withdraws an earlier decision, or leaves a question open, that is the record of how the reading was actually reached, and it is left standing — a conjecture that is visible can be checked, and one that has been tidied away cannot.

The English never silently repairs the page. A construable but wrong reading is rendered literally and the conjecture is recorded here, never applied to the text. How the English is produced and checked →

Translators' notes (kt method, 2026-10-27), moved out of the running English. Each is keyed by chunk, column and band, and quotes the English words it follows. Plate verdicts: translate/maximus-opuscula/IMAGE-CHECK.tsv. Glossary: translate/maximus-opuscula/GLOSSARY.md. In the English, ⟨…⟩ marks words we supply where the printed sentence needs them (whether the plate lost them or Migne's text never had them); each has a note below.

Chunk 0001 (mo-001)

  • 0009 · …the Confessor, to Marinus the most holy presbyter. — P. Marinus, presbyter of Cyprus, Maximus's correspondent in the monothelite controversy and the addressee of several Opuscula (1–3, 10, 19–20). Singular addressee: thou*.
  • 0009 · …for the establishment of an unerring union , — T. The bracket is Combefis's alternative reading, γνώσεως ("knowledge") for ἑνώσεως ("union"); his Latin likewise prints unionem [scientiam]. The scaffold sets the closing bracket three lines later, after ποθοῦντος; it belongs here (OCR table). Scholion 1 below reads ἑνώσεως.
  • 0009 · …the person of reason, and reason the nature — T. "Person" (πρόσωπον) and "nature" (φύσις): Scholion 2 below glosses them as hypostasis and essence. Combefis's Latin adds ac substantia and ac essentia to make the same point.
  • 0012 · …and the likeness of God who created him; — S. Gen 1:26 (image and likeness); the new man, Eph 4:24; Col 3:10.
  • 0012 · …the opponent of falsehood, the other of wickedness. — T. "Opponent" renders ἀντίπαλον, whose second half is lost at the line end in the scaffold (ἀντί- / Ἐντεῦθεν); supplied from the Latin aemula and the parallel at the start of the next chunk (ἀντίπαλον).
  • 0012 · …and insist that Christ has one elective will — T. The eight terms the opponents treat as one are held apart throughout the piece: θέλημα will, θέλησις volition, βούλησις wish, βουλή counsel, προαίρεσις choice, γνώμη inclination (the scholars' "gnomic" will), ἐξουσία authority, δόξα opinion, φρόνησις prudence. προαιρετικὸν θέλημα = "elective will".
  • 0012 · …all the idioms essentially present to the nature. — S. Cf. the definitions of will gathered in Opusc. 26–27. The chain of definitions that follows (wish, counsel, choice, what we deliberate about) draws on Nemesius, De natura hominis 33–34, and behind him Aristotle, Nicomachean Ethics 3.2–3. "In our power" renders τὰ ἐφ᾿ ἡμῖν throughout.
  • 0013 · …choosing is fitting is wishing also altogether fitting. — T. So printed, and Combefis's Latin agrees (Non omnibus quibus convenit eligi, omnino etiam convenire voluntate appeti). The examples show the converse: wishing extends to things that cannot be chosen.
  • 0013 · …thing deliberated, for example the mode of health. — T. Read τρόπον ("mode") for the scaffold's τόπον ("place"), with the Latin paranda sanitatis modus.
  • 0016 · …choice is none of these considered by itself: — T. Οὐδέτερον, properly "neither of two", is said of three members; so printed, and rendered "none".
  • 0017 · …since we deliberate neither about all men , — T. The bracket is Combefis's alternative, τῶν ἀνθρωπίνων ("all things human"), which his Latin follows (quae hominis sunt).
  • 0017 · …of habit to operation with respect to choice. — T. "Holds the ratio" renders λόγον ἐπέχει: inclination stands to choice as habit stands to operation.
  • 0020 · …twofold, rational and irrational; but choice is changeable. — T. So printed (τρεπτή, Latin mutabilis). The contrast set up by the "twofold" of opinion is not spelled out.
  • 0020 · …ever-moving is immortal: the soul therefore is immortal. — S. Cf. Plato, Phaedrus 245c.
  • 0020 · …as opinion. Of prudent thought, that is, prudence. — T. φρόνημα = "prudent thought", held apart from φρόνησις = "prudence"; the four-term set (φρόνησις, φρονητόν, φρονητικόν, φρόνημα) is defined at the start of the next chunk.

Chunk 0002 (mo-002)

  • 0021 · …a habit opposed to both folly and over-subtlety; — T. "Over-subtlety" renders ὑπέρνοια, the excess opposed to ἄνοια ("folly"), as prudence is the mean between them; Latin nimiae prudentiae ac sagacitati.
  • 0021 · …from it, they say, the uttered word proceeds. — T. The ladder of the mind is held term by term: νόησις intellection, ἔννοια concept, ἐνθύμησις pondering, φρόνησις prudence, διαλογισμός reasoning, ἐνδιάθετος λόγος inward word, προφορικὸς λόγος uttered word. S. Cf. the same ladder in John of Damascus, Expositio fidei 36 (2.22).
  • 0021 · …the one capable of prudence, and prudent thought. — T. φρόνησις prudence, φρονητόν the object of prudence, φρονητικόν the one capable of prudence, φρόνημα prudent thought: the set parallels νόησις, νοητόν, νοητικόν, νόημα.
  • 0021 · …and prudent thought. And prudence is the relation; — T. Read ἡ σχέσις ("the relation") for the scaffold's ἢ; Latin habitudo est.
  • 0021 · …realities, but only a display of bare names. — T. ὑπόστασις here in its wider sense, real existence, kept as "hypostasis" by the glossary rule.
  • 0024 · …elective, and capable of impulse and of use. — T. "Capable of reasoning" and "capable of consideration" render λογιστικός and σκεπτικός, both mangled in the scaffold (καὶ-γιστικὸς, σκτι-κὸς) and restored from the chain just set out and the Latin (ratiocinandi … considerandi).
  • 0024 · …makes the passions contrary to nature subsist parasitically: — T. The pair λόγος–τρόπος, "principle and mode": the principle of virtue is the natural powers rightly used, the mode of misuse is what gives the passions a parasitic subsistence (παρυφιστῶν) beside nature.
  • 0025 · …as the end foreconceived before all the ages; — S. Cf. Eph 1:4; 3:11.
  • 0025 · …each, those who have grown together with him. — S. Eph 1:23 ("filled in all"); Eph 4:7 (the measure of grace); Rom 12:6 (the proportion of faith); 1 Cor 12:27 (members).
  • 0025 · …same with it in natural quality and quantity — T. The connective is damaged at the line break (εἰσί- / .εὶ τε); read εἰσί· τῷ τε with the second witness and the Latin (nec cum voluntate per naturam bona … humana voluntas … idem exstiterit). The clause stands under the "as long as … not" that governs the series.
  • 0028 · …be shown sick straightaway with the Manichaean fantasy, — P. Mani (3rd c.) and his followers held that Christ's body was an appearance; "fantasy" is the charge of docetism.
  • 0028 · …madness of Arius and the essence-fusion of Apollinarius, — P. Arius (d. 336), who made the Son a creature; Apollinarius of Laodicea (d. c. 390), who denied Christ a human mind and was charged with fusing the Word and the flesh into one essence (συνουσίωσις, "essence-fusion").
  • 0028 · …And if elective, which they call of inclination, — T. προαιρετικόν = "elective", γνωμικόν = "of inclination"; Maximus here treats the opponents' two names as one.

Chunk 0003 (mo-003)

  • 0029 · …Christ, like a mere man according to Nestorius, — P. Nestorius, bishop of Constantinople (428–431), condemned at Ephesus; the charge is that he made Christ a man joined to God.
  • 0029 · …as of other counsel and of other inclination. — T. ἑτερόβουλος and ἑτερόγνωμος, "of other counsel and of other inclination" (βουλή counsel, γνώμη inclination).
  • 0029 · …own choice, present in God incarnate by appropriation. — T. κατ᾿ οἰκείωσιν, "by appropriation" (glossary § VII; Latin arrogatio): Christ makes our choice his own as he makes our dishonour his own, without its being his by nature.
  • 0029 · …teacher Thalassius concerning the difficulties of holy Scripture, — P. Thalassius, abbot in Africa, to whom Maximus addressed the Quaestiones ad Thalassium. S. Cf. Ad Thalassium 42, on the "unchangeableness of choice" in Christ.
  • 0032 · …dishonour by appropriation, in his love for man; — T. "Penalty" renders ἐπιτιμία: the blameless passions (hunger, death) that came upon the race as the penalty of sin, which Christ received in fact; the passions "of dishonour" (our sin and its blame) he took on by appropriation only.
  • 0032 · …declare that he is not soulless and mindless; — T. Read ἄψυχος ("soulless") for the scaffold's ἄψιλος, with the Latin anima ac mente careret and the pairing with ἄνους.
  • 0033 · …chapter of the Difficulties of the great Gregory, — S. Ambigua ad Ioannem 7 (PG 91.1068–1101), on Gregory Nazianzen, Or. 14.7; the "one operation of God and the saints" is at PG 91.1076C.
  • 0033 · …of the great Gregory, the account is clear. — T. Combefis's Latin sets a marginal heading here (Una Dei et sanctorum operatio, id est, deificatio …) that has no Greek counterpart; not rendered.
  • 0033 · …one another: what is able, power, the possible. — T. δυνάμενον, δύναμις, δυνατόν: "what is able, power, the possible". πρᾶξις is held as "practice" by the glossary; here it means the actualization of a power.
  • 0033 · …makes the lovers of good things by adoption — T. θέσει, "by adoption", against φύσει, "by nature".
  • 0036 · …having come about by grace for those deified. — T. Migne prints καὶ τὰ χάριν (confirmed on the plate), which does not construe; the English reads κατὰ χάριν, "by grace", a conjecture (Latin per gratiam).
  • 0036 · …whose essential principle is sown in the nature. — T. Read οὐσιώδης (singular, with λόγος) for the scaffold's οὖσιώδεις; Latin essentialis … ratio.
  • 0036 · …is Christ from two natures, Godhead and manhood; — T. "From two natures" (ἐκ δύο φύσεων) followed by "in which he is known" (ἐν αἷς) and "which he is" (αἷς): Maximus holds all three relations, and the prepositions are kept exactly.
  • 0036 · …only-begotten Word and Son and Lord of glory; — S. 1 Cor 2:8.
  • 0037 · …all things, and through whom, and to whom, — S. Rom 11:36.
  • 0037 · …the "according to the likeness", life, is hypostasis; — S. Gen 1:26; cf. the opening of the letter, where life is called the person of reason and reason the nature of life.
  • 0037 · …through him we live and move and are"; — S. Acts 17:28, printed Δι᾿ αὐτοῦ ("through him"); Acts reads Ἐν αὐτῷ ("in him"), and Combefis's Latin gives In ipso. The Greek is translated as printed.
  • 0037 · …deification. Mayest thou too hear these things, Nilus; — P. Nilus, not otherwise identified; apparently a reader whom the scholiast addresses.

Chunk 0004 (mo-004)

  • 0040 · …50 Of the same, to the same Marinus. — P. Marinus, presbyter of Cyprus, Maximus's correspondent (Opusc. 1, 7, 10, 19–20).
  • 0040 · …the treatise on operations and wills, chapter 50.* — T. The treatise "on operations and wills" is lost; Opusc. 2 and 3 are its chapters 50 and 51. The numeral is garbled in the Kraken text (see OCR table); 50 by the table and by the following piece's "ch. 51".
  • 0040 · …on operations and wills, chapter 50. That Severus — P. Severus, Monophysite patriarch of Antioch (512–518, d. 538), whose formula is "one composite nature".
  • 0040 · …things united into one essence after the union, — T. συναίρεσις = coalescence (glossary § V). Manes (Mani), Apollinarius of Laodicea and Eutyches stand for phantasm, confusion and the merging of the natures.
  • 0040 · …Ebion, of Paul of Samosata and of Nestorius, — P. Ebion (eponym of the Ebionites) and Paul of Samosata (bishop of Antioch, deposed 268) are named with Nestorius as dividers who made Christ a mere man inspired by grace.
  • 0041 · …introduced in operation the division of the things; — T. κατ᾿ ἐνέργειαν "in operation", here and in the next clause, is opposed to κατὰ τὴν ἐπίνοιαν "in conception", that is, "in act, in reality" (Latin revera). The glossary word is kept so that the reader sees the term.
  • 0041 · …uncut and undivided quantity of the things differing, — T. The plate's ποιότητα "quality" cannot stand: the argument at once runs difference → quantity → number. Combefis's Latin has the same slip and corrects it in brackets (qualitatem [quantitatem]). Read ποσότητα.
  • 0041 · …the natures in a quality of inclination alone, — T. γνωμικός "of inclination": γνώμη = inclination throughout (glossary § II).
  • 0041 · …of which Nestorius asserted the union to be, — T. Authority (αὐθεντία), worth (ἀξία) and identity of volition are the Nestorian terms for a union of persons by relation rather than by hypostasis.
  • 0045 · …account of identity of counsel devised by him, — T. ταυτοβουλία, "identity of counsel" (βουλή = counsel): Nestorius's word, as the Latin says, for a moral union of two willing persons.

Chunk 0005 (mo-005)

  • 0045 · …having understood with the movements of the mind — T. νοῦ κινήμασι διανοήσαντες: the dative is taken with the participle ("having understood with the movements of the mind"). Myriobiblos reads τοῦ for νοῦ, which would make it the inclination "of some man" moved by his own mind; Combefis's mentis ex sententia motum has the noun and takes it with the man. The Greek order allows either; the English keeps the Greek order.
  • 0048 · …all the idioms essentially present to the nature; — T. A definition of natural will: the appetitive power, and the power "holding together" (συνεκτική) the idioms of the nature. Cf. the definitions of Opusc. 1.
  • 0048 · …will. «For his willing», says the great Gregory, — S. Gregory Nazianzen, Or. 30 (Theological Oration 4), 12.
  • 0048 · …been deified, plainly, by growing-together with what deifies; — T. συμφυΐα = growing-together (Latin conjunctione ac quasi naturali cohaesione), here and in Opusc. 6: the growing-together of the human willing with the divine.
  • 0048 · …the other passions, the contraction before death too, — T. συστολή, "contraction" (glossary), is the shrinking from death; the Latin renders metum ac fugam, "fear and flight". Its opposite here is ὁρμή, impulse.
  • 0048 · …having put death to death by the flesh, — T, S. The plate as read has θανατώσας τὸν θάνατον, "having put death to death". Myriobiblos has a feminine article, and Combefis renders carne peccatum interimens, "putting sin to death by the flesh", which would be τὴν ἁμαρτίαν (cf. Rom 8:3). The English follows the Greek as read; a check at the plate is owed.
  • 0048 · …let not my will but thine be done»; — S. Matt 26:39 (cf. Luke 22:42).
  • 0049 · …of Apollinarius, and of the co-essentiation of Eutyches. — T. συνουσίωσις, "co-essentiation": the fusing of the natures into one essence (Latin essentiarum conflatum).
  • 0049 · …of the party of Severus disputing with me; — P. Maximus's stay in Crete, on his way from Constantinople to Africa (c. 626–632). The Severians' words are given in their own first person ("we do not confess"), as the Greek has them.
  • 0049 · …two operations, according to the Tome of Leo, — P. The Tome of Leo: Pope Leo I's letter to Flavian (449), received at Chalcedon (451), "each form operates in communion with the other what is proper to it".
  • 0052 · …Sion, you who inhabit the daughter of Babylon.» — S. Zech 2:6–7 LXX (2:10–11 Hebrew numbering).
  • 0052 · …accordingly an equal reciprocation of the things related. — T. ἀντιστροφή, "reciprocation": the logical conversion of relatives (Latin reciprocationem). "Conversion" is kept for ἐπιστροφή and μεταβολή.
  • 0053 · …the one, again without counsel and without hypostasis; — T. ἄβουλος, πολύβουλος, ἀπειρόβουλος, ἑτερόβουλος: compounds of βουλή, held to "counsel" (glossary § VI), although the argument is about θέλημα. The Latin has voluntas throughout.

Chunk 0006 (mo-006)

  • 0056 · …asked by letter about the mystery of Christ.* — P. George, presbyter and abbot, otherwise unknown. T. ἡγούμενος = abbot (glossary § III).
  • 0057 · …it above matter and the things about it, — T. The Kraken text has πα ταύτην, a broken word; read περὶ ταύτην, "the things about it" (Latin a terrenis rebus). Unsure: μετὰ or παρὰ are possible.
  • 0057 · …and who, by the emptying of the Word, — S. Phil 2:7.
  • 0057 · …former thou mayest display the mortification of Jesus, — S. 2 Cor 4:10.
  • 0057 · …of the God-fashioning, after the manner of man, — T. θεοπλαστία κατ᾿ ἄνθρωπον, "God-fashioning after the manner of man": the forming of the manhood by God the Word (Latin divinae secundum hominem … formationis).
  • 0060 · …undefiled bloods of the all-pure Mother of God; — T. Θεομήτωρ, "Mother of God", kept apart from Θεοτόκος (Theotokos).
  • 0060 · …as having acquired its nod toward either side; — T. ῥοπή, "nod" (glossary § V): the will's leaning either way.
  • 0060 · …this world comes, and finds nothing in me» — S. John 14:30, as Maximus cites it (εὑρίσκει for ἔχει).
  • 0060 · …be unsubjected in respect of what is ours, — S. Gregory Nazianzen, Or. 30.5: Christ "unsubjected" (ἀνυπότακτος) in respect of what is ours, until he makes us subject.
  • 0061 · …was thought so by some, from his saying: — S. Gregory Nazianzen, Or. 30.12, on John 6:38.
  • 0061 · …proclaiming thee in turn, according to his promise, — S. Matt 10:32.
  • 0064 · …prevails over the human: thus one must answer.* — T. κατ᾿ ἐπικράτειαν, "by prevailing": the opponents' formula, that the divine operation so masters the human that only one operation can be named. Opusc. 5 is three short refutations; the second and third headings are rendered in italics as printed.
  • 0064 · …Godhead and the manhood there is one operation.»* — T. The instrument–mover image is the Monenergists' (the Word moving the flesh as its instrument); it is printed as a quoted formula.
  • 0065 · …resists and wrestles», as the divine Gregory says: — S. Gregory Nazianzen, Or. 30.12, quoted as in Opusc. 4 above. Migne prints τῷ Θεῷ ("God") here, where the same citation reads τῷ θείῳ ("the divine") earlier in this piece and just below; the English follows the print.
  • 0065 · …what I will, but let thy will prevail», — S. Cf. Matt 26:39; Luke 22:42. The verb ἰσχυέτω, "prevail", is not the Gospels'; it is the form in which the saying is argued here.
  • 0065 · …of contraction, or of courage? Of utmost convergence, — T. σύννευσις, "convergence" (Latin consensionis): the human will's inclining together with the divine.
  • 0068 · …which, and which the same was by nature. — T. "From which, and in which, and which" (ἐξ ὧν καὶ ἐν αἷς … καὶ ἅπερ): the Chalcedonian formula of the natures of which Christ is, in which he is known, and which he is.
  • 0068 · …the destruction of what was willed by God, — T. θέλησις θελήματος, "the volition of the common will", construes, and the Latin has velis. But four lines later the same thought is τὴν τοῦ κοινοῦ [θ]έσιν … θελήματος, "the positing of the common will", and θέσις may be the reading here too. A check at the plate is owed.
  • 0068 · …nature, and deified it whole by the essentiation. — T. οὐσιοῦσθαι / οὐσίωσις, "be essentiated / essentiation": the Word's taking on being in the whole human nature (Latin totam totum Verbum naturam vere induerit).

Chunk 0007 (mo-007)

  • None · …tome sent to Cyprus, to Marinus the deacon.* — P. Marinus, deacon of Cyprus (later presbyter), the addressee of Opusc. 1–3, 10 and 20; deacon here in the Greek and in Combefis's Latin alike.
  • 0072 · …and again thou enlightenest from the everlasting mountains — S. Ps 75:5 (LXX), «thou enlightenest wonderfully from the everlasting mountains».
  • 0072 · …up against those who walk the royal road — S. Num 20:17; 21:22.
  • 0073 · …great God and Saviour of all, Christ himself, — S. «Face to face», 1 Cor 13:12; «the great God and Saviour», Titus 2:13.
  • 0073 · …truly assuming from the holy Mother of God, — T. Θεομήτωρ, "Mother of God", kept apart from Θεοτόκος (Theotokos).
  • 0073 · …to himself according to hypostasis, properly and indissolubly, — T. ἀδιασπάστως / ἀδιάσπαστος: "indissolubly / indissoluble" throughout, kept apart from ἀχώριστος (inseparable) and ἀδιαίρετος (undivided).
  • 0076 · …cut in half, upward and downward, by expansion, — T. The subject is the one composite will (so Scholion 1, Opusc. 7 end). κατὰ διαστολήν: the glossary's "expansion", the will spread out towards the two extremes, "upward" to God and "downward" to us; Combefis: secundum distinctionem, and he renders ἄνω τε καὶ κάτω as quod susdeque jactant.
  • 0077 · …things themselves, and having put the new Ekthesis — P. The Ekthesis: Heraclius's edict of 638, drafted by Sergius of Constantinople, which forbade speaking of one or two operations and confessed one will in Christ.
  • 0077 · …divine guide of the Nyssenes and great Gregory. — S. Gregory of Nyssa; the sentence is cited under his name in the dyothelite florilegia; locus not identified here.
  • 0080 · …against the cause of nature; nor even inclination, — T. γνώμη: "inclination" throughout, kept apart from θέλησις (volition) and βούλησις (wish).
  • 0080 · …war in the things that are, for revolt — T. στάσις here is civil strife, hence "revolt", not the "rest" of the triad movement–rest.
  • 0080 · …man: Not mine, but thy will be done; — S. Luke 22:42; cf. Matt 26:39.

Chunk 0008 (mo-008)

  • 0081 · …himself, Not my will be done, but thine, — S. Luke 22:42.
  • 0081 · …one will», according to the most wise Cyril. — S. Cyril of Alexandria; cited under his name in the dyothelite florilegia; locus not identified here.
  • 0081 · …discourse On his Incarnation and on the Trinity, — S. Ps.-Athanasius, De incarnatione et contra Arianos 21 (PG 26.1021), quoting Matt 26:39, 41.
  • 0081 · …willing is in nothing contrary, being wholly deified.» — S. Gregory Nazianzen, Or. 30.12 (the fourth theological oration, the second On the Son), whose text adds Θεῷ: «contrary to God».
  • 0081 · …wholly, throughout, deified by its convergence and growing-together — T. συμφυΐα: "growing-together", here and below, beside περιχώρησις (interpenetration).
  • 0084 · …ours, as the wise Cyril has taught us; — S. Cf. Cyril of Alexandria, Commentary on John, on John 12:27.
  • 0084 · …theandric operation of Dionysius the revealer of God, — S. Ps.-Dionysius, Ep. 4, «a certain new theandric operation». Combefis's note on the facing Latin: Dionysius said καινήν τινα, "a new", not μίαν τινά, "one", as Cyrus altered it.
  • 0084 · …forth through both, of the most wise Cyril, — S. Cyril of Alexandria, Commentary on John IV, on John 6:53 (PG 73.577), on the raising of Jairus's daughter: «showing one and kindred operation through both».
  • 0085 · …is it that says an operation is hypostatic?) — T. Scholion 3 below names Nilus as the one who spoke of a hypostatic operation.
  • 0085 · …dead and healing every disease and every infirmity, — S. Matt 4:23; 9:35.
  • 0088 · …of the flesh, teach as doctrine the co-essentiation — T. συνουσίωσις: "co-essentiation"; συνουσιωμένον in Part 1 is "co-essentiated".
  • 0088 · …«the one incarnate nature of God the Word», — S. Cyril of Alexandria, Ep. 46 (second to Succensus), and elsewhere: the formula of Severus and his party.
  • 0089 · …the ages of ages. Amen. SCHOLIA 1. Explanation. — T. The heading of each scholion is printed «Εr.», Latin letters standing for a Greek abbreviation, read here as Ἑρ(μηνεία), "explanation"; Combefis's Latin omits it.
  • 0089 · …the division equal to it. 3. Explanation. Nilus. — P. Nilus: perhaps Nilus of Ancyra (d. c. 430), the ascetic writer; the scholiast gives no more.

Chunk 0009 (mo-009)

  • None · …the saints, on the two operations in Christ. — T. The heading's first word, ἼΣΟΝ ("copy"), was read by the machine as the Latin EXEMPLUM; Combefis's Latin: Epistola missa a sancto Maximo ad sanctissimum episcopum dominum Nicandrum, de duabus in Christo operationibus.*
  • 0089 · …and all-blessed Father, having, as thy name bears, — P. Nicander, a bishop, his see unknown. φερωνύμως, "as thy name bears": Νίκ-ανδρος joins νίκη (victory) and ἀνήρ (man), hence "manliness" and "victory" in this sentence.
  • 0089 · …received from him, in hope, the unfading crown — S. 1 Pet 5:4.
  • 0092 · …if anyone else, delighting in the Lord himself: — S. Ps 36:4 (LXX), «delight thyself in the Lord».
  • 0092 · …soul, which are like fingers of the hands, — S. Cf. Ps 143:1 (LXX), «who teacheth my hands for battle, my fingers for war».
  • 0092 · …those who exalt themselves against the Lord Almighty — S. Job 15:25.
  • 0092 · …operation feed the passions of dishonour like swine — S. The far country and the swine, Luke 15:13–15; «passions of dishonour», Rom 1:26.
  • 0092 · …of the world, no, nor ever shall be, — S. Matt 24:21; the Truth by nature is Christ, John 14:6.
  • 0092 · …through practice in a place of green pasture, — S. Ps 22:2 (LXX), «in a place of green pasture … by the water of rest».
  • 0092 · …his own and life-giving blood, according to volition — S. Cf. Acts 20:28; ἁρμοσάμενον, "espoused", recalls 2 Cor 11:2.
  • 0093 · …us, the things that characterize our nature essentially; — T. Migne prints σώζοντα (confirmed on the plate), which does not agree with the genitive absolute it stands in (αὐτοῦ τοῦ σαρκωθέντος Λόγου … μετασκευάσαντος … δείξαντος); the English reads σώζοντος, a conjecture.
  • 0093 · …pass entirely into non-existence, as not very good, — S. Gen 1:31.
  • 0093 · …by union, in the very Word and Wisdom? — T. The same words twice: made by λόγος and σοφία, preserved in the Λόγος and Σοφία himself; "reason" and "Word" render one Greek word.
  • 0096 · …is there any positing of it at all.» — S. Ps.-Dionysius, cited under his name; locus not identified here.
  • 0096 · …to Leo, the celebrated pope of the Romans, — S. Leo I, Tome to Flavian (Ep. 28.4): «each form does what is proper to it, in communion with the other».
  • 0097 · …say it with fair words — fine thinking, — T. καλοφροσύνη, "fine thinking", ironic, as the apology «I do not know how to say it with fair words» shows; the facing Latin is damaged at this point.

Chunk 0010 (mo-010)

  • 0100 · …is set apart and separated from the extremes. — T. "The extremes" (τῶν ἄκρων) here are the Father and us: a will proper to the hypostasis would set the Son apart from both.
  • 0100 · …account, God forbid, do we deny the singular — T. The noun is understood with μοναδικάς; the Latin supplies voces, and the Greek gives φωνάς a few lines below. φωνή is rendered "expression" throughout this piece, "voice" where it is Christ's bodily utterance.
  • 0100 · …I mean the operations: both the «theandric operation» — S. Dionysius the Areopagite, Ep. 4, to Gaius (PG 3.1072C): καινήν τινα τὴν θεανδρικὴν ἐνέργειαν.
  • 0100 · …the «one and kindred operation shown through both». — S. Cyril of Alexandria, In Ioannem 4 (on John 6:53–54; PG 73.577C), quoted in full in Opusc. 9 below. T. συγγενής = "kindred" throughout (Latin affinis, cognata); συγγένεια = "kindredness".
  • 0100 · …on account of the union, and the growing-together — T. συμφυΐα = "growing-together" (Latin cohaesio), συμφυῶς = "as grown together"; glossary § VII.
  • 0100 · …divine and at the same time the manly — T. ἀνδρικός "manly": the second member of θεανδρικός (θεός + ἀνήρ), kept apart from ἀνθρώπινος "human".
  • 0100 · …in nature. For since he had said before, — S. Maximus's expanded paraphrase of Dionysius, Ep. 4 (PG 3.1072B–C), whose kernel is «not doing the divine things as God, nor the human things as man»; the explanatory clauses are Maximus's.
  • 0101 · …show that both the flesh has become God — T. As printed, and the Latin agrees (carnem Deum): the flesh "God", the operation "divine".
  • 0104 · …with which it had philosophized the things there». — S. Gregory Nazianzen, Or. 7.21, On Caesarius.
  • 0104 · …he who is witnessed by the kindred Spirit». — S. Gregory Nazianzen, Or. 39.16, On the Holy Lights.
  • 0104 · …man; and by water, in turn, the body.» — S. Cyril of Alexandria, In Ioannem 2.1 (on John 3:5); cf. PG 73.245.
  • 0104 · …to what is one by union or ; — T. Migne prints τὸ καθ᾿ ἕνωσιν, ἢ δηλονότι σκοπούντων (confirmed on the plate), with nothing after the "or"; the Latin reads quod vel unione vel natura unum exsistit, and the sense needs the second member. The words in square brackets are NOT in Migne: the English supplies [κατὰ φύσιν ἓν] from the Latin, a conjecture; a phrase seems lost.
  • 0104 · …in «One from both, and through one both»; — S. Cf. Gregory Nazianzen, Or. 37.2; not located exactly.
  • 0104 · …and one nature of God the Word incarnate»; — S. Cyril's formula, Ep. 46 (2 ad Succensum), with "one hypostasis" added as Maximus cites it; the Latin has una persona.
  • 0104 · …yet not by nature, but by the concourse». — S. Cf. Gregory Nazianzen, Or. 37.2. σύνοδος = "concourse" (glossary § V).
  • 0104 · …Light, and light, and light, but one light»; — S. Gregory Nazianzen, Or. 31.3.
  • 0104 · …one nature, the highest», says Gregory the Theologian. — S. Cf. Gregory Nazianzen; not located.
  • 0105 · …breaking the loaves, and distributing them to them; — S. Matt 26:26.
  • 0105 · …Matthew, in the second book of the Confession: — T. τῆς ἐξομολογήσεως "of the Confession", as printed; the Latin agrees (libro secundo Confessionis). The passage is from Cyril on Matthew 12:28 (cf. the fragments of his Commentary on Matthew, PG 72); the title is left as Migne prints it.
  • 0105 · …that he operates in the Spirit of God. — S. Matt 12:28.

Chunk 0011 (mo-011)

  • 0108 · …to be of an operation through the Spirit.» — T. The close of the quotation from Cyril on Matthew begun at the end of the previous chunk.
  • 0108 · …the Spirit.» So the Father has spoken truly, — T. "The Father" is Cyril, whose words Maximus now paraphrases.
  • 0108 · …indissoluble, maintaining in unitary fashion the utmost growing-together — T. συμφυΐα = "growing-together", as in the previous chunk; ἑνοειδῶς = "in unitary fashion".
  • 0109 · …had his own operation by habit and privation, — T. καθ᾿ ἕξιν καὶ στέρησιν: Aristotle's pair of possession and privation, rendered with the glossary's "habit" (Latin secundum habitum et privationem).
  • 0112 · …clarity, nor a well-ordered character for the accomplishing — T. Read ἐξάνυσιν "accomplishing" for the printed ἐξάνεσιν "relaxation"; the Latin has ad ea quae virtutis sunt expedienda.
  • 0112 · …multitudes of my sins. And I entreat you — T. The close addresses its recipients in the plural (ὑμῶν, ὑμᾶς, ἐπισκόπους), so "you", not thee.
  • 0112 · …has made you overseers and saviours of souls, — S. Acts 20:28.
  • 0112 · …show yourselves mediators of God rather than imitators, — T. As printed, μεσίτας Θεοῦ μᾶλλον … ἢ μιμητάς, "mediators of God rather than imitators"; Combefis renders non magis Dei sequestres quam imitatores, "no less imitators than mediators". A note call stands at μᾶλλον, and a word may be lost. The English follows the Greek.
  • 0112 · …the weakness of me, the poor and needy, — S. Cf. Ps 39:18; 85:1 (LXX), πτωχὸς καὶ πένης.
  • 0112 · …the assumption, one and one from two contraries», — S. Gregory Nazianzen, Or. 38.13 (= Or. 45.9): Θεὸς προελθὼν μετὰ τῆς προσλήψεως, ἓν ἐκ δύο τῶν ἐναντίων. The doubled ἓν καὶ ἕν is printed, and the Latin agrees (unum et unum), so it is kept.

Chunk 0012 (mo-012)

  • 0112 · …Christ-loving island of the Sicilians, abbots and monks, — T. ἡγούμενοι = "abbots" (glossary § III). The piece has no Greek title of its own: the address line is the heading, and Combefis's Latin title leaked into the Greek just above it (logged in mo-011).
  • 0113 · …for them», said David, the ancestor of God, — T. Θεοπάτωρ "ancestor of God": David as forefather of Christ according to the flesh.
  • 0113 · …to the last object of appetite by nature, — T. τῇ τοῦ ἐσχάτου φύσει … ὀρεκτοῦ: God as the last (ultimate) object of natural appetite; ὀρεκτόν from ὄρεξις = appetite.
  • 0113 · …another. For which reason I too, your servant, — T. Read ὑμετέρῳ "your" for the printed ἡμετέρῳ "our"; the Latin has servo vestro.
  • 0113 · …cause one of the little ones to stumble.» — S. Gregory Nazianzen; not located (cf. the apologetic orations, Or. 2 and 36). The close alludes to Matt 18:6.
  • 0113 · …time two (that is, according to them, three) — T. The charge Maximus rebuts is that he held "one" (the hypostatic operation of the whole) together with "two" (the natural ones), which his opponents tally as three. The "third operation" in what follows is that hypostatic operation, as his opponents name it.
  • 0116 · …it, is the incarnate Word of one operation, — T. ὁμοεργής = "of one operation" (Latin ejusdem operationis); ὁμοεργία = "oneness of operation".
  • 0116 · …different; and from this their own supposition polytheist, — T. The Greek has no verb: κἀντεῦθεν πολύθεον τὴν οἰκείαν ὑπόληψιν. "They make" is supplied from the sense; the Latin paraphrases (sequitur eos plures deos sentire).
  • 0116 · …are known to hold the same as Sabellius, — P. Sabellius, third-century teacher who made Father and Son one hypostasis; Arius, below, the Alexandrian presbyter condemned at Nicaea (325), who divided the Son from the Father in essence.
  • 0117 · …not even those who fashion goat-stags would conceive. — T. The goat-stag (τραγέλαφος), the school example of a composite that does not exist.
  • 0120 · …say both that God is passible against sin, — S. Cf. Gregory Nazianzen, Or. 30.1 (θεὸς παθητὸς κατὰ τῆς ἁμαρτίας).
  • 0120 · …to say, co-divine», according to Gregory the Theologian; — S. Gregory Nazianzen, Or. 45.13: γενομένη ὅπερ τὸ χρῖσαν, καί, τολμῶ λέγειν, ὁμόθεον. T. ὁμόθεος = "co-divine".
  • 0120 · …Theologian; «neither doing the divine things as God», — S. Dionysius, Ep. 4 (PG 3.1072C), as paraphrased in Opusc. 8 above.
  • 0120 · …incarnate Word was not a paying of penalty, — T. ἔκτισις, payment of a debt or penalty (Latin poenae solutio); κένωσις = self-emptying (Phil 2:7).
  • 0120 · …the resurrection, or the taking-up, or the cleansing, — T. Signs done "toward others": ἀνάστασις, ἀνάληψις, κάθαρσις may here mean the raising of the dead, the restoring of the sick and the cleansing of lepers. The Latin has resurrectio, assumptio, leprosi mundatio.
  • 0120 · …could contain the books that should be written.» — S. John 21:25.
  • 0121 · …the expressions about Christ, writing thus to Acacius: — P. Acacius, bishop of Melitene, to whom Cyril wrote (433) defending the reunion with the Antiochenes.
  • 0121 · …man; and others hold a certain middle order"?» — S. Cyril of Alexandria, Ep. 40, to Acacius of Melitene (PG 77.181–201). T. The close of the quotation is printed after Maximus's question; placed here with the Latin (logged).
  • 0121 · …witness about these things, defending the Eastern bishops, — P. John of Antioch and the bishops of the East, reconciled with Cyril by the Formula of Reunion (433).
  • 0124 · …whom are all things, and we by him. — S. 1 Cor 8:6. T. Printed ἐξ αὐτοῦ, "of him", where the New Testament has εἰς αὐτόν, "unto him"; kept as Cyril's text (the Latin agrees, ex ipso).
  • 0124 · …flesh; showing one and kindred operation through both."» — S. Cyril of Alexandria, In Ioannem 4 (on John 6:53–54; PG 73.577C).
  • 0124 · …suspect; if indeed he himself declares to Hermias, — P. Hermias, the addressee of Cyril's Dialogues on the Trinity and of the dialogue On the Incarnation. The quotation runs on into the next chunk.

Chunk 0013 (mo-013)

  • 0125 · …confessed to be also of the same essence.» — S. The close of a testimony whose opening stands in the preceding chunk; cf. Cyril of Alexandria, Thesaurus 32 (on the identity of operation and essence).
  • 0125 · …is necessary: either to bring in the teacher — T. "The teacher" is Cyril of Alexandria, whom the monenergists claimed; Maximus argues that read their way Cyril would contradict his own Thesaurus.
  • 0125 · …saying, «showing one and kindred operation through both»; — S. Cyril of Alexandria, Commentary on John 4 (on John 6:53–54), on the miracles worked by word and by touch.
  • 0125 · …bring forward the letter of Heraclianus to Achillius, — P. Heraclianus, bishop of Chalcedon (6th c.), anti-Severan writer; Achillius, the addressee, is otherwise unknown.
  • 0125 · …movement and nature of the parts by appropriation; — T. κατ᾿ ἀναφοράν "by reference" (Latin secundum rationem / relationem); κατ᾿ οἰκείωσιν "by appropriation" (glossary § VII; Latin secundum arrogationem), the technical Christological sense.
  • 0128 · …they not bring in twelve operations and passions, — T. Latin agrees: duodecim operationes passionesque.
  • 0128 · …later for a cause, that is, our salvation; — S. Cf. Gregory Nazianzen, Or. 29.19 (what he was "without cause", what he became later "for a cause").
  • 0129 · …is united to God, this is also saved,» — S. Gregory Nazianzen, Ep. 101.32 (to Cledonius); "he who is named for theology" is Gregory the Theologian.
  • 0129 · …unreasonable objection of those who are unequally yoked, — S. Cf. 2 Cor 6:14 (ἑτεροζυγοῦντες).
  • 0129 · …more simple, namely of the letter to Marinus — P. Marinus, presbyter of Cyprus, addressee of Opusc. 1, 7, 10, 20; the letter here disowned is a forgery circulated under Maximus's name.
  • 0129 · …by us, and of the letter to Pyrrhus, — P. Pyrrhus, patriarch of Constantinople 638–641, Sergius's successor and Maximus's opponent in the disputation of 645.
  • 0129 · …that I destroyed, I make myself a transgressor,» — S. Gal 2:18; "the vessel of election" is Paul (Acts 9:15).
  • 0132 · …greatest salvation, if, having embraced it, he confessed — T. The bracketed optative ὁμολογήσειεν is printed beside the indicative ὡμολόγησεν as an editorial correction; the English gives both.
  • 0132 · …praise, not accepting the principle of our economy — T. οἰκονομία here in its pastoral sense: the accommodation of the praise to win Pyrrhus.
  • 0132 · …you like men, and be strong in spirit, — S. 1 Cor 16:13; cf. Luke 1:80.
  • 0132 · …spirit, not turning aside to right or left — S. Cf. Deut 5:32.
  • 0132 · …am the way, and the life, and truth*; — S. John 14:6 (the Greek order as printed: way, life, truth, and ἀλήθεια without the article); the royal road, cf. Num 20:17.

Chunk 0014 (mo-014)

  • 0133 · …letter to the lord Marinus, presbyter of Cyprus. — P. Marinus, presbyter of Cyprus (see Opusc. 1, 7, 20). T. The Greek addresses him now in the singular (θεοτίμητε πάτερ, σύ), now in the plural of respect (γεγράφατε, ὑμετέρων, ὑμῖν); the English keeps thou throughout, the addressee being one man. The heading's Greek word is Ἴσον, "copy" (Latin Exemplum*).
  • 0133 · …the mediocrity, and magnifying by poverty the cheapness, — S. Cf. Phil 2:7 (κένωσις) and 2 Cor 8:9 (πτωχεία): Marinus's condescension figured as Christ's.
  • 0133 · …above all, by the clarifying of thy prayers — T. ὑμετέρων, plural of respect (note 1).
  • 0133 · …the synodical of the present most holy Pope — P. Pope Theodore I (642–649); "the queen of cities" is Constantinople (Latin glosses Constantinopolitani).
  • 0136 · …the ancestral sin». And for the first, they — T. "They" = the Romans, defending the pope's synodical letter.
  • 0136 · …laboured by him on the holy evangelist John; — S. Cyril of Alexandria, Commentary on John (cf. on John 16:14–15).
  • 0136 · …in order to show the going-forth through him, — T. The Romans' word, as Maximus reports it, is προϊέναι, "go forth", kept apart from ἐκπορεύεσθαι, "proceed", which is the Father's alone: the Spirit proceeds from the Father and goes forth through the Son.
  • 0136 · …chapters that follow, I have read: a draft — T. σχέδει, read as σχεδίῳ, "draft" (OCR table); Latin libellum.
  • 0136 · …have read: a draft of Theodore of Pharan; — P. Theodore, bishop of Pharan in Sinai (early 7th c.), the first literary witness to monenergism.
  • 0136 · …having shaped and dictated almost the whole Ekthesis — P. The Ekthesis (638), the monothelite exposition of faith issued under Heraclius, drafted by Sergius.
  • 0136 · …respects he somehow overshadowed and darkened the account, — T. κατὰ ταῦτα λόγον, without article; Latin in hunc modum sermonem. Translated as the account (λόγον) of the hypostasis.
  • 0137 · …did he come to my nothingness in Carthage. — P. Byzacium, the province south of Carthage in Roman Africa; Maximus writes from Carthage (c. 645–646).
  • 0137 · …as possible, I have sent . The economy — T. οἰκονομία here is the practical arrangement for having the volume copied; the glossary word is kept.
  • 0137 · …attack against the one who set it out, — T. ὁ ἐκθέμενος, "the one who set it out", is the author of the commentary on John being copied.
  • 0137 · …the same. From the letter written in Rome. — T. The heading is damaged in the Kraken text (OCR table); read from the Latin Ex epistola Romae scripta*. A fragment, Maximus writing at Rome (after 646).
  • 0137 · …and holy doctrines, as the six holy synods, — T. "Six synods" in Greek and Latin alike, though Maximus elsewhere counts five (Opusc. 9 above); the fragment may postdate 649 or be retouched.
  • 0140 · …who believe in him: this, by two words, — T. "Two words": presumably the monothelite formula itself, or the two imperial edicts (Ekthesis and Typos).
  • 0140 · …two words, without labour and without death , — T. The bracketed καμάτου, "toil", is printed as a correction of θανάτου, "death"; the English gives both.

Chunk 0015 (mo-015)

  • 0141 · …same holy Maximus, written to Peter the illustris. — P. Peter, illustris and general in Africa (addressee of Letters 13–14); the excerpt survives only in Anastasius the Librarian's Latin (Collectanea), so the source here is Latin. T. Defloratio*, "extract".
  • 0141 · …to Peter the illustris. By thy praiseworthy syllables — T. Vestris … vos … vobis … discite: the Latin's plural of respect to one man; the English keeps thou throughout (Peter is also addressed in the singular, dignatus es, tuo, benedicte domine mi*).
  • 0141 · …to thine unworthy servant concerning the abbot Pyrrhus. — P. Pyrrhus, patriarch of Constantinople 638–641, deposed; in Africa and then at Rome (645–646) he sought reception without anathematizing his doctrine; Maximus calls him "abbot", the rank left to him.
  • 0141 · …cause, I should hasten to thy most renowned — T. famosissimus, probably for Greek ἐνδοξότατος; kept "most renowned" throughout.
  • 0141 · …their deeds and a reception without success . — T. sine successu receptionem is what the plate prints, so the difficulty is in the Latin, not the scan: it does not construe as a wish (perhaps Anastasius mistranslated, or cum successu was meant). Rendered as printed, marked [?].
  • 0141 · …heretics, is the new faith and exposition (Ἔκθεσις). — T. The Latin glosses expositio with the Greek word, garbled in the OCR (table). The Ekthesis (638) of Heraclius, drafted by Sergius.
  • 0141 · …he may punish it more justly), his predecessor — P. Sergius, patriarch of Constantinople 610–638, author of the Ekthesis; "this man" is Pyrrhus.
  • 0141 · …it; and again those who came after them — T. et rursus posteriores horum: the plate prints rursus, "again" (the scan read rius).
  • 0141 · …cultivate it to the contempt of the Godhead. — T. divinitatis, taken as for θεότης, "Godhead".
  • 0142 · …For they whisper here and there that Sophronius, — P. Sophronius, patriarch of Jerusalem 634–638, the first opponent of the Alexandrian Pact; Maximus's teacher.
  • 0142 · …as thou thyself bearest witness, most praiseworthy one, — T. ut ipsi testamini, laudatissimi: plural verb and vocative plural, as the plate prints; the plural of respect to Peter, cf. note 2.
  • 0142 · …For they were reducing him who then ruled — P. The emperor Heraclius (610–641).
  • 0143 · …of holy memory, pope of the elder Rome. — P. Pope John IV (640–642), to whom Heraclius wrote in 641 disowning the Ekthesis as Sergius's work.
  • 0143 · …they convict themselves, that these most excellent men — T. fortissimi, ironic; probably for Greek κράτιστοι.
  • 0143 · …become of the mind of the apostolic see; — T. nec sensus apostolicae facti sunt sedis, literally "did not become of the sense of the apostolic see": they took no account of it, or did not share its mind. Wording uncertain.
  • 0143 · …impious Ekthesis they had taken the great Honorius — P. Pope Honorius I (625–638), whose letter to Sergius the monothelites cited for one will.
  • 0143 · …from their own heresy? Indeed, the holy Arcadius — P. Arcadius, archbishop of Cyprus (d. c. 641/642).
  • 0143 · …who came after this one, the holy John? — P. Severinus (pope 640) and John IV (640–642).
  • 0143 · …he who now presides, the most blessed pope, — P. Theodore I (642–649).
  • 0143 · …to prefer the heavenly faith to their own. — T. The plate prints suae nequaquam passi sunt cœlestem fidem praeferre: "to their own" supplies the noun the Latin leaves understood (their own opinion).
  • 0143 · …at once upon the first reading (for Cyrus — P. Cyrus, patriarch of Alexandria (631–642), whose Pact of Union of 633 with the Theodosians, in nine chapters, taught one theandric operation.
  • 0143 · …were plainly the doctrines of the impious Apollinarius. — P. Apollinarius of Laodicea (4th c.), who denied Christ a human mind.
  • 0143 · …weeping, after the manner of a second Jeremiah, — S. Cf. Lam 2:11 and Jer 8:21 (contritio filiae populi mei).
  • 0144 · …not to call Pyrrhus most holy or Almificus. — T. Almificum, capitalised on the plate and kept as a title, "Almificus"; a rare Latin honorific, probably for a Greek one.
  • 0144 · …synods, according to the sacred canons and definitions, — T. terminos, rendered as for ὅρους, "definitions".
  • 0144 · …heavenly powers, binds and looses also in heaven. — S. Matt 16:19; 18:18.
  • 0144 · …show the cleanness of his conduct to others — T. The plate prints tantum inutiliter … aliis et privatis hominibus: "to others and to private men" (the scan read miis).
  • 0144 · …more abundantly, whether he is willing to assent — T. annuere, "assent" (scan anaucre); the sentence runs on at the head of p. 89, cols 145–146, above Opusc. 13, where the piece ends.

Chunk 0016 (mo-016)

  • 0145 · …holy Maximus. On the two natures of Christ. — T. The author line is garbled in the Kraken text (τοΥ Αγιον MλΞοΥ-), read ΤΟΥ ΑΓΙΟΥ ΜΑΞΙΜΟΥ; the line under it (SAΣΤΗ ΜΑΧi) is a leaked Latin head, and the title is confirmed by the Latin De duabus Christi naturis*.
  • 0145 · …Father and the Son, and affirms him God. — T. Migne prints καὶ Θεὸν πῶς διαβεβαιοῖ (confirmed on the plate), which does not construe ("and affirms him how God"); the Latin has ac Deum esse affirmat. The English gives the sense the Latin gives and marks the word; ὄντως, "truly", is a conjecture only.
  • 0148 · …their not being each of its own hypostasis, — T. ἰδιοϋπόστατον, "of its own hypostasis": neither the Word nor the flesh has a hypostasis apart from the other in the union (Latin quod non propria cujusque hypostasis est). Note that ch. 6 addresses a plural you (ἐκφωνεῖτε), ch. 7 a singular (λέγεις … λέγε): kept.
  • 0149 · …the difference destroy the union according to hypostasis; — S. Cyril of Alexandria, the Twelve Anathematisms (Ep. 17, to Nestorius), esp. 3 (against those who divide the hypostases after the union); cf.
  • 0149 · …difference in the one of the holy Trinity. — S. The Council of Chalcedon (451), definition of faith: «the difference of the natures being in no way destroyed on account of the union»; cf.
  • 0149 · …it, and is not contemplated by bare conception. — T. ἐνυπόστατος, "enhypostatic": two definitions follow, the species really subsisting in its individuals, and the nature co-subsisting with another in one hypostasis. ἐπίνοια = conception (kept apart from λογισμός, thought).
  • 0149 · …same as it also according to the hypostasis; — T. The last word of the printed line is the negative οὐ (the OCR gave οὗ, a breathing slip; confirmed on the plate 2026-11-06): the definition just given (same essence, different hypostasis), the parallel clause and the Latin (non etiam idem est … secundum hypostasim) all require it.
  • 0152 · …also really possesses the common of the essence. — T. ἐνούσιον, "the enousial", a term formed on ἐνυπόστατον: what really possesses the common of the essence. Kept as a term of art.
  • 0153 · …impulse and movement of thought toward either side. — T. θέλημα γνωμικόν, "will of inclination". ἐφ᾿ ἑκάτερα, "toward either side", that is, either of two alternatives.

Chunk 0017 (mo-017)

  • 0153 · …that the intrusive Ekthesis of Heraclius the emperor, — P. The Ekthesis ("Exposition") of faith, issued by the emperor Heraclius in 638 and drafted by Sergius: it forbade speaking of one or of two operations and confessed one will in Christ.
  • 0153 · …at the suggestion of Sergius, president of Constantinople, — P. Sergius, patriarch of Constantinople (610–638), the architect of monenergism. T: πρόεδρος, "president", is kept as the Greek title.
  • 0156 · …to Stephen, the most holy bishop of Dora, — P. Stephen, bishop of Dora in Palestine, subject to the see of Jerusalem ("the holy city of Christ our God"), sent to Rome by Sophronius. T: the letter, though addressed to Stephen, speaks to plural addressees (θεολαμπεῖς καὶ πανάριστοι, ὑμῶν): rendered you throughout.
  • 0156 · …the one is taken and the other left. — S. Luke 17:34–35; cf. Matt 24:40–41.
  • 0156 · …and the other left. And to be taken — T. The plate prints αἴροντα with Migne's correction [αἴρεσθαι] beside it; the English follows the correction, which the pairing αἴρεσθαι … ἀφίεσθαι requires (Latin assumendum).
  • 0156 · …assumed it. «For what is unassumed is unhealed,» — S. Gregory Nazianzen, Ep. 101 (first to Cledonius).
  • 0157 · …If, transgressing the commandment, we transgressed by volition, — T. Migne prints δίχα θελήσεως, ἀλλ᾿ οὐ θελήσεως δίχα (confirmed on the plate), "apart from volition, and not apart from volition", which contradicts itself; the Latin has voluntatis nutu non sine voluntate. The English reads διὰ θελήσεως, "by volition", a conjecture.
  • 0157 · …should know, and he could not be hid;* — S. Mark 7:24, as Maximus quotes it (without the main verb ἀπῆλθεν).
  • 0157 · …morrow he willed to go forth into Galilee. — S. John 1:43. Printed ἐλθεῖν, "to come"; read ἐξελθεῖν, "to go forth", with the Latin exire* and with Maximus's own recapitulation below (Ἤθελε ἐξελθεῖν).
  • 0160 · …all, infinitely, infinite times over. So too , — T. ἡ Γραφή stands in square brackets in Migne, an editorial supply; kept so.
  • 0160 · …great voice. For the one named for immortality, — T. Athanasius, whose name means "immortal" (ἀθανασία).
  • 0160 · …his discourse on the incarnation and the Trinity: — S. Ps.-Athanasius, De incarnatione et contra Arianos 21 (cf.); Matt 26:39, 41.
  • 0161 · …Theology, in his second discourse on the Son: — S. Gregory Nazianzen, Or. 30 (the fourth Theological Oration, the second on the Son), 12; John 6:38.
  • 0161 · …inhabited world, in his discourse on the Pascha: — S. Gregory of Nyssa, on the Pascha (cf. In Christi resurrectionem, the oration De tridui spatio); Mark 1:42; the loaves, Matt 15:32–36.
  • 0161 · …of those he laboured against Eunomius the impious: — S. Gregory of Nyssa, Contra Eunomium (cf.); 2 Cor 5:19.
  • 0161 · …again the same says, in his against Apollinarius: — S. Gregory of Nyssa, Antirrheticus adversus Apolinarium (cf. ch. 32); Matt 26:39. συναφές, "conjoinedness", is kept apart from συνάφεια, conjunction.
  • 0164 · …horse-races, and again our assembly has become smaller: — S. John Chrysostom, Contra Anomoeos hom. 7 (De consubstantiali*), PG 48; Matt 26:39; John 10:30. "The queen [city]" is Constantinople.
  • 0164 · …teaches in the twenty-fourth chapter of the Thesaurus: — S. Cyril of Alexandria, Thesaurus 24 (PG 75); Matt 26:39. The quotation runs on into the next chunk.

Chunk 0018 (mo-018)

  • 0165 · …to die, because the flesh naturally refuses death.» — S. The close of Cyril of Alexandria, Thesaurus 24, whose quotation opens at the end of the previous chunk; Matt 26:39.
  • 0165 · …, commenting on the great evangelist John, says: — S. Cyril of Alexandria, Commentary on John (cf. on John 12:27). T: Οὗτος δέ, "this same [Father]"; the Latin has Sed et. ἀνεθέλητος = "unwilled" of death, "without volition" of a nature (mo-017).
  • 0165 · …the inspired Severian, who was bishop of Gabala, — P. Severian, bishop of Gabala in Syria (d. after 408), a preacher at Constantinople.
  • 0165 · …and on Father, save me from this hour: — S. Matt 26:39; John 12:27 (printed ἀπό for ἐκ); within the quotation John 12:27, Matt 26:38, Matt 26:41. The homily is cf. Severian, on "Father, if it be possible".
  • 0165 · …second book to Gratian, goes through these things: — S. Ambrose, De fide II.8 (cf. §§ 69–70), quoted in Greek; Phil 2:6.
  • 0168 · …Lord made the water wine, whose beginning is: — S. Cyril of Jerusalem, homily on the wedding at Cana (John 2:1–11), known in fragments; cf. T: as printed, the incipit runs straight on into the excerpt inside one quotation; so also the widow homily below.
  • 0168 · …saints, against Nestorius and Eutyches of evil name: — S. Leo I, Tome to Flavian (Ep. 28), 4. P: Flavian, bishop of Constantinople (d. 449).
  • 0168 · …two mites into the Treasury, whose beginning is: — S. Ps.-Chrysostom, on the widow's mites (Mark 12:41–44; Luke 21:1–4); cf.
  • 0168 · …discourse on Thomas the apostle, against the Arians: — S. Ps.-Chrysostom, on Thomas the apostle (cf.); John 20:27. T: γνώμη is "inclination" even here ("the doubtful inclination").
  • 0168 · …Alexandrians, in the thirty-second chapter of the Thesaurus: — S. Cyril of Alexandria, Thesaurus 32 (PG 75). ποίημα = "thing made", kept apart from κτίσμα, creature.
  • 0169 · …preserved.» These things, then, these men proclaim piercingly, — T. Printed σίδε, and at the close of the Monophysite testimonies οἶδε; both read οἵδε, "these men", the verb understood (Latin isti).
  • 0169 · …with them division altogether, as being made known — T. Migne prints γνωριζούσης with his correction [γνωριζομένης]; the English follows the correction.
  • 0169 · …opposing party the reverse. For Apollinarius the impious — P. Apollinarius of Laodicea (d. c. 390), who held that the Word took the place of the human mind in Christ.
  • 0169 · …the divine incarnation in the likeness of man: — S. Apollinarius, Apodeixis*, and the work on the divine incarnate manifestation (fragments, ed. Lietzmann); cf.; Matt 26:39.
  • 0169 · …to the word of truth, Polemon the heretic, — P. Polemon, a disciple of Apollinarius (late fourth century); "true to his name" plays on πόλεμος, war.
  • 0172 · …other deified, as Gregory the Cappadocian has it, — S. Cf. Gregory Nazianzen, Or. 30.12 (θεωθὲν ὅλον) and Ep. 101: the formula "one divine, the other deified" which Polemon rejects.
  • 0172 · …of the letters written by him to Timothy, — P. Timothy, Apollinarian bishop of Berytus.
  • 0172 · …the leader of the folly of the Agnoetae, — P. Themistius, deacon of Alexandria (sixth century), a follower of Severus of Antioch and founder of the Agnoetae, who held that Christ's human knowledge was ignorant of some things. Severus (d. 538) was the chief theologian of the opponents of Chalcedon.
  • 0172 · …the heresiarch of the heretics named after him, — P. Theodosius, anti-Chalcedonian patriarch of Alexandria (535–566), whose followers were called Theodosians; Theodora, wife of Justinian.
  • 0172 · …the same ill-met heretic again, in chapter 52, — T. Migne prints νβʹ, "52" (confirmed on the plate; the Kraken text lost the ν and gave βʹ).
  • 0172 · …evil-minded and contentious, who plundered of the Mopsuestians, — P. Theodore of Mopsuestia (d. 428).
  • 0173 · …the womb itself the inward appropriation toward him.» — S. Matt 8:3. εὐδοκία = good pleasure; οἰκείωσις = appropriation (glossary § VII).
  • 0173 · …accursed, in what he calls the Clear Initiation, — T. Printed Ἐμφανεῖ μιήσει, at the clipped left margin of the page; read μυήσει, "initiation" (Latin clara institutione). The work is lost; the reading of its title is a doubt.
  • 0173 · …pseudo-deacon of the most foul heresy of Nestorius, — P. Paul the Persian, a Nestorian writer; otherwise little known.
  • 0173 · …and the doctrines before them — the Ekthesis, — T. The plate, and Combefis's Latin (unio), read ἕνωσις, "union"; the sense, and the quotations of the Ekthesis that follow, want ἔκθεσις. The English reads Ekthesis.
  • 0173 · …confess one will of our Lord Jesus Christ»; — S. The Ekthesis of Heraclius (638), as Maximus quotes it.

Chunk 0019 (mo-019)

  • 0176 · …two wills: the one human, the other divine.» — S: Athanasius, cf. De incarnatione et contra Arianos 21 (PG 26.1021), on Matt 26:39; "as we have set out before" points back to an earlier chunk of this tome.
  • 0176 · …not even contrary to God, being wholly deified.» — S: Gregory Nazianzen, Or. 30.12.
  • 0176 · …conjoinedness of his own Godhead with the Father.» — S: Gregory of Nyssa, cf. Antirrheticus adversus Apollinarium; the last testimony comments Luke 22:42 ("Not my will, but thine"). The four are given as separate sayings; none located here exactly.
  • 0176 · …nature apart from sin, and with great abundance.» — S: John Chrysostom, cf. the homily on "Father, if it be possible" (Matt 26:39; PG 51.31–40). Not located exactly.
  • 0176 · …unwilled, because of the flesh naturally refusing death.» — S: Cyril of Alexandria, cf. Commentary on John (on John 12:27); not located exactly.
  • 0176 · …two wills: the one divine, the other human.» — S: Severian of Gabala, a homily on the agony; not located.
  • 0176 · …be one operation where the essence is different?» — S: Ambrose, De fide II.8.70 (Quomodo enim una operatio diversae est substantiae?).
  • 0176 · …suffering as man, operating as God, the same.» — S: Cyril of Jerusalem, cf. Catecheses; not located.
  • 0176 · …other, what it has had as its own.» — S: Leo, Tome to Flavian (Ep. 28.4): Agit enim utraque forma cum alterius communione quod proprium est.
  • 0176 · …runs together; and so I perceived two operations.» — S: John Chrysostom; not located. ἐλεημοσύνη, "mercy" (Combefis eleemosynam): Christ's acts of mercy, where the two operations run together.
  • 0176 · …God and of the thing made is one.» — S: Cyril of Alexandria, cf. Thesaurus 32 (PG 75.453).
  • 0177 · …to be impious, and alien to Christian doctrine. — T: "it" throughout is the Ekthesis (feminine participles in the Greek); the author is "he". "Their glorious confession": Migne prints αὐτῶν (the Kraken text's πότῶν is a misreading).
  • 0177 · …which is impious and alien to Christian doctrine.» — S/P: the Ekthesis of Heraclius (638), drafted by Sergius of Constantinople, quoted closely; its infinitive construction ("[we hold] that the expression … scandalizes") is rendered as a statement.
  • 0177 · …be one, and having no difference in willing.» — P: Apollinarius of Laodicea (see note 14, Opusc. 15, part 2); this and the following sayings of heretics come from a florilegium; not located.
  • 0177 · …in willing.» Polemon, the enemy of the truth: — P: Polemon (see note 16, Opusc. 15, part 2); "enemy" (πολέμιος) plays on his name.
  • 0177 · …one at war with himself.» Themistius the lawless: — P: Themistius (see note 19, Opusc. 15, part 2); "lawless" (ἀθέμιτος) plays on his name.
  • 0177 · …one, as also the volition.» Theodore the God-stricken: — P: Theodore of Mopsuestia (see note 22, Opusc. 15, part 2); "God-stricken" (θεόπληξ) plays on his name.
  • 0177 · …one and the same authority.» Nestorius the all-stricken: — T: "all-stricken" (πάμπληξ) continues the play of the previous epithet.
  • 0177 · …be one.» Paul, a Persian in inclination too: — P: Paul the Persian (see note 25, Opusc. 15, part 2); Persian "in inclination too", that is, Nestorian in mind as in birth.
  • 0180 · …two wills, but to confess identity of will, — T: printed αὐτοβουλίαν ("self-will"), and Combefis's Latin column prints the same; the Ekthesis's own word, and Maximus's at col. 192 (ταυτοβουλίαν), is ταυτοβουλία, "identity of will", which the gloss "that is, one will, of the two persons" requires. English follows ταυτοβουλίαν; logged as print.
  • 0180 · …will, of the two persons fashioned by him. — S: the Ekthesis, paraphrased (Nestorius "taught identity of will of the two persons fashioned by him").
  • 0181 · …monks or laymen, they are to be anathematized.» — S: Council of Chalcedon (451), closing clause of the Definition (ACO II.1.2, 130).
  • 0181 · …who piously confess him — would still choose — T: the printed ἔτ᾿ ἂν [αἱροῖτ᾿ ἂν] carries the verb in Migne's square brackets, an editorial supplement.
  • 0181 · …Wherefore, beholding it in the facts themselves, master, — T: Δέσποτα, singular, although the closing paragraph addresses a plural ("you", ὁδηγήσατε, ὑμᾶς); kept as printed (Latin Domine).
  • 0181 · …who have reached the ends of the ages: — S: 1 Cor 10:11; "the last beast", cf. Rev 13.
  • 0181 · …keeping his own followers from every alien opinion, — T: "opinion" renders [δόξης], bracketed in Migne as a supplement.
  • 0184 · …me, your own, who limp on both hams, — S: 3 Kgdms 18:21 (LXX): χωλανεῖτε ἐπ᾿ ἀμφοτέραις ταῖς ἰγνύαις.

Chunk 0020 (mo-020)

  • 0184 · …two wills of the one Christ our God. — T: the heading names no addressee; "sanctified Father" is singular throughout (thou*) and is not named.
  • 0184 · …transferred thy thought to the life without interval, — T: ἀδιάστατος, "without interval" (Latin indivisam): the life without break or division, the contemplative life.
  • 0185 · …to what is dissolving and to the world — T: Migne prints κόσμου [κοσμικοῦ], the second word in square brackets, an editorial alternative for the first ("the world" or "worldly"); the English gives both, the bracketed one in brackets.
  • 0185 · …its paths with knowledge, thou showest in operation — T: κατ᾿ ἐνέργειαν, "in operation", that is, in act (Latin ipso opere).
  • 0185 · …to search out the depths of the Spirit, — S: cf. 1 Cor 2:10 ("the depths of God").
  • 0185 · …who reveals the wisdom spoken in a mystery — S: 1 Cor 2:7.
  • 0185 · …definition of natural will which the devout monk — P: the "devout monk" whose definition of natural will is examined is not named in the piece.
  • 0185 · …is one will and that of inclination another, — T: τὸ γνωμικὸν θέλημα, "will of inclination".
  • 0188 · …as the thing willed or what is willed. — T: θεληθέν, "the thing willed"; θελητόν, "what is willed": the opponents' two words for the object, kept apart throughout.
  • 0188 · …what else is this but a circular demonstration, — T: διάλληλος δεῖξις, the circular proof, in which each term is shown through the other (Latin reciproca indicatio).
  • 0188 · …them back to the one begetting and willing. — S: Gregory Nazianzen, Or. 29.6, on will and generation as "middle" between the one willing or begetting and what is willed or begotten.
  • 0188 · …into identity, and declare these to be one?» — T: Migne's quotation marks run from Οὐ γὰρ ἕπεται to διαγορεύειν, and Combefis's Latin does the same; Gregory's own words are probably only "it does not altogether follow" (cf. Or. 29.6), the rest Maximus's question. Kept as printed.
  • 0188 · …thing willed is something among the things outside. — T: the Greek prints πῶς [πρὸς] δέ, with πρός in square brackets, Migne's correction of πῶς; Combefis's Latin has Ad haec. English follows πρὸς δέ, "moreover"; logged as print.
  • 0192 · …natural operation, even though it is with burning, — S: the iron in fire, a standing image since Origen, De principiis II.6.6. T: the verb after καύσεως is blotted on the plate (κα[?]ται), perhaps καθορᾶται, "is beheld", parallel to ὁρᾶται of the nature; the sense is not in doubt.
  • 0192 · …made flesh, more than perfect and before perfection, — S: ὑπερτελὴς καὶ προτέλειος, cf. Dionysius, Divine Names 2.10. T: the infinitive clause ("and thereby to attest …") hangs on the preceding question, as its consequence.
  • 0192 · …and its law: a deviation and a slip; — T: after ὀλίσθημα the plate shows only a faint tail of letters, illegible; the next printed line (p0114.16 in the machine text) is show-through from the verso, not text, and the three Latin lines Combefis sets across the page here are not part of the Greek.
  • 0192 · …to speak more properly, the multiplicity of will; — T: ταυτοβουλία, "identity of will" (the Ekthesis's word for Nestorius); πολυβουλία, "multiplicity of will", Maximus's correction of it.
  • 0193 · …him; the will of inclination he marked off, — T: "marked off", that is, distinguished and excluded from Christ (Latin dispunxit); Migne prints ἀποδιώρισε (confirmed on the plate; the Kraken text has ἀποιώρισε).
  • 0193 · …no means opposition, which is for their dissolution. — T: Migne prints a bracketed [ἀλλὰ] (Kraken [ἀλὰ]) before πρὸς τὴν τούτων διάλυσιν; it does not construe, and the English renders without it.

Chunk 0021 (mo-021)

  • 0196 · …nature, and the privations of what injures it. — T: ἕξις and στέρησις, "habit" and "privation", Aristotle's pair (Categories 10): the will aspires to possess what constitutes the nature and to be deprived of what harms it.
  • 0196 · …and, Not what I will, but whatever thou; — S: Mark 14:36 (οὐ τί ἐγὼ θέλω, ἀλλὰ τί σύ).
  • 0196 · …the natural agony for a little while , — T: the Latin here is fuller, naturali tantisper mortis formidini ejusque anxio timori cedens, "yielding for a little to the natural fear of death and its anxious dread"; a participial phrase seems lost between p0116.53 and p0117.01. [?] marks it; logged as doubt.
  • 0197 · …and saying: Not mine, but thine be done: — S: Luke 22:42.
  • 0197 · …times to have made a reflection of willing, — T: ἔμφασις, the glossary's "reflection": here an outward showing of the faculty.
  • 0197 · …to have done this long before the passion. — S: cf. Matt 26:38; John 12:27 ("my soul is troubled").
  • 0197 · …once flesh of God the Word, animate, rational», — S: the formula ἅμα σάρξ, ἅμα Θεοῦ Λόγου σάρξ, ἅμα σὰρξ ἔμψυχος λογική, cited under Athanasius's name in the dyothelite florilegia; not located in his genuine works.
  • 0197 · …account of our sins, and not of doctrines; — P: probably the Arab conquests of the 640s, which Maximus elsewhere reads as chastisement for sin.
  • 0197 · …by themselves at all, but what is random. — T: τὸ εἰκαῖον, "what is random", that is, what is without ground (Latin debile quidpiam vanumque).
  • 0200 · …being, it will bring into a parasitic subsistence. — T: παρυποστήσει, from παρυπόστασις, the "parasitic subsistence" of what has no being of its own (cf. Dionysius, Divine Names 4.31).
  • 0201 · …united to it in nothing else but operation. — T: at the foot of col. 200 (p0118.58) the text carries a rubric, Ὅτι οὐκ ἔστιν ὑποστατικὴ ἐνέργεια, "That there is no hypostatic operation": a marginal summary, not part of the running text, and not rendered here.
  • 0201 · …in the experience of human things, with authority; — T: ἐν τῇ σαρκὸς προσβολῇ τῶν θείων: the divine operation shown when the flesh sets about divine works (Latin qua caro, quae divina essent, aggrediebatur); the human shown in his undergoing human things "with authority", that is, of his own will.
  • 0201 · …the subaltern genera to the most specific species, — T: Porphyry's ladder: γενικώτατον γένος, "most generic genus"; ὑπάλληλα γένη, "subaltern genera"; εἰδικώτατον εἶδος, "most specific species".

Chunk 0022 (mo-022)

  • 0204 · …this reason, according to the most wise Cyril, — S: cf. Cyril of Alexandria, Scholia de incarnatione Unigeniti 1, on the name «Christ» as neither a definition nor a signifier of essence.
  • 0204 · …this is to do violence to the hypostasis. — T: ἀνενέργητος = without operation; ἀνυπόστατος = without hypostasis; ἐπηρεάζειν = "to do violence to". The opponents argue that a nature "not without hypostasis" must be a hypostasis; Maximus answers that it is enhypostatic (col. 205).
  • 0204 · …itself is naturally contemplated, to be a relation. — T: the sentence is the subject of Scholion 1 below, which explains it on the pair invisible / visible: denying the privative yields the correlate affirmed, not the relation itself. At the end of the clause Migne prints τὸ γεννητὸν ἢ ὁρατόν, "the visible" (confirmed on the plate 2026-11-06; the ἀόρατον earlier in the same line belongs to the first pair).
  • 0204 · …so that they may not merely pronounce it? — T: Migne prints ἀλλὰ [ἵνα] μὴ ἀποφήνωνται μόνον, the bracket an editorial supplement; rendered with it. Combefis's Latin reads differently, vel solum nominare prohibuit ("forbade even to name them").
  • 0205 · …hypostasis an essence, but presents it as enousial, — T: ἐνούσιος, coined on the pattern of ἐνυπόστατος, rendered "enousial" (existing in an essence); ἐνύπαρκτος likewise "enhyparctic" (having real existence, ὕπαρξις); ἐνεργός "operant", beside ἐνεργητικός "operative"; ἐνδύναμος "potent".
  • 0205 · …same, by saying «every divine and human operation» — S/P: the formula of Sergius of Constantinople's Psephos (633), repeated in the Ekthesis of Heraclius (638): one and the same Christ operates «every divine and human operation».
  • 0209 · …likewise tempted according to volition, without sin alone. — S: Heb 4:15.
  • 0209 · …orthodox confession of our great God and Saviour — S: Tit 2:13.
  • 0212 · …to those who confess him unashamedly before men, — S: cf. Matt 10:32; Mark 8:38.
  • 0212 · …our all-glorious and all-holy, immaculate Lady, the Theotokos — T: Θεοτόκος, "God-bearer", kept as Theotokos.
  • 0212 · …Mary, and of all the saints. Amen. SCHOLIA — T: the two scholia comment on col. 204 (the invisible and the visible) and col. 205 (the converted argument on essence and hypostasis). The Kraken heading ΣχΟλα is ΣΧΟΛΙΑ.

Chunk 0023 (mo-023)

  • 0212 · …Of the same. On the definitions of demarcations. — T: διαστολή, which the Latin renders distinctio, discretio, is "demarcation" throughout, held apart from διάκρισις (distinction), διαίρεσις (division) and διαφορά (difference). The heading's ΤΟΥ ΑΥΤΟΥ is mangled in the Kraken text.
  • 0212 · …against, that is, being divided or being destroyed. — T: the pair is ἐναντίωσις (opposition, glossary) and τὸ ἀντικεῖσθαι (being set against); Maximus glosses them at once as division and destruction, and the scholion repeats the gloss. Aristotle's own usage (ἀντικείμενα = opposites, ἐναντία = contraries) runs the other way; the glossary word is kept.
  • 0213 · …these are manifestly destructive of one another. SCHOLION — T: the scholion is in Byzantine twelve-syllable verse (Ἐναντίωσιν, τὴν διαίρεσιν νόει·); rendered as prose.
  • 0213 · …barley, and the like. Union according to fusion: — T: συναλοιφή, "fusion" (Latin coalitio), held apart from συναίρεσις (coalescence).
  • 0216 · …rhetor and synodicarius of Paul, archbishop of Constantinople. — P: Theodore of Byzantium, deacon and rhetor, a monothelite of the court of Paul II of Constantinople (patriarch 641–653); synodicarius*, an officer of the patriarchal synod. The Latin caption THEODORI BYZANTINI MONOTHELITAE leaked into the Greek is not rendered.
  • 0216 · …is contemplated in Christ not according to appropriation — T: κατ᾿ οἰκείωσιν, the technical "appropriation" of the Christological argument (Latin secundum arrogationem): Christ's making his own what is not his by nature. Rendered "appropriation", and οἰκειοῦσθαι "make one's own", as ruled in glossary § VII ("kinship", § V, is for the ascetic sense only).
  • 0216 · …to the already condemned heresy of the Agnoetae. — P: the Agnoetae, followers of Themistius, deacon of Alexandria (sixth century), who held that Christ was ignorant according to his manhood; condemned by Eulogius of Alexandria and Pope Gregory I.
  • 0216 · …on the Son, and others in other writings. — S: Athanasius, Orationes contra Arianos III.42–50; Gregory Nazianzen's "first oration on the Son" is Or. 29 (Theological Oration 3), though his treatment of the ignorance is in Or. 30.15, which Maximus cites below as the second.
  • 0217 · …above by Theodore, deacon and rhetor. Solution 1.* — P: Marinus, presbyter of Cyprus, Maximus's correspondent in several of the Opuscula.
  • 0217 · …of their ingratitude: 'My God and your God'; — S: John 20:17. "Greater": John 14:28. "He created": Prov 8:22. "He sanctified": John 10:36.
  • 0217 · …'greater'; 'he created'; 'he sanctified'», and what follows. — S: Gregory Nazianzen, Or. 29.18.
  • 0217 · …your God', and 'greater', said of the Father, — T: παρὰ τοῦ Πατρὸς εἰρημένον, "said of (with respect to) the Father" (Jn 14:28, "the Father is greater than I"); Combefis's Latin reads quod a Gregorio dictum est.
  • 0220 · …of the divine will, the ignorance as well. — T: κατὰ τοῦ θείου θελήματος, rendered "in respect of the divine will"; Latin una cum divina voluntate.
  • 0220 · …things according to nature for us, and suffered. — S: cf. Gregory Nazianzen, Or. 30.15–16 and Or. 29.20, where the natural things of the incarnate God are listed.
  • 0220 · …for them to leave to Apollinarius and Manes? — P: Manes (Mani, d. 276), founder of Manichaeism, whose Christ had no real flesh.
  • 0221 · …one separates what appears from what is understood?» — S: Gregory Nazianzen, Or. 30.15.
  • 0224 · …with us, those who believe in him orthodoxly, — T: the Greek has the accusative τοὺς εἰς αὐτὸν ὀρθοδόξως πιστεύοντας after ἡμῖν; construed, with the Latin (nobiscum, qui recta fide in eum credamus), as in apposition to "us".

Chunk 0024 (mo-024)

  • 0224 · …same holy Maximus, solution of the second absurdity.* — T: the second of Theodore's two difficulties (Opusc. 18, col. 216–217): that "natural wills" is a phrase the Fathers never used. Maximus answers it here, again to Marinus.
  • 0224 · …to it naturally, also the deprecating of death.» — S: a chain of patristic sayings without separate lemmas, which Maximus assigns jointly to Athanasius, Basil, Gregory, John (Chrysostom), Theophilus and Cyril of Alexandria; not located individually. «He says» (φησι) is singular in the Greek.
  • 0225 · …is, personal wills, shrinking from the natural ones, — T: Migne prints δ... ούμενοι [δυσωπούμενοι] (confirmed on the plate), a lacuna with the editor's supplement; rendered with the supplement ("shrinking from", "abashed at").
  • 0228 · …of all the natural things, through the appropriation, — T: διὰ τῆς οἰκειώσεως, "through the appropriation", the opponents' κατ᾿ οἰκείωσιν of Opusc. 18 (see the note there).

Chunk 0025 (mo-025)

  • 0228 · …20 — DOGMATIC TOME TO MARINUS THE PRESBYTER — T. Our Greek gives only the author line (ΜΑΞΙΜΟΥ ΤΟΥ ΑΓΙΩΤΑΤΟΥ ΜΟΝΑΧΟΥ ΚΑΙ ΟΜΟΛΟΓΗΤΟΥ); the line after it is Combefis's Latin heading leaked into the Greek, and no Greek title naming the addressee stands in the text. The short title is Combefis's. P: Marinus, presbyter of Cyprus, Maximus's correspondent in several Opuscula; addressed in the singular throughout.
  • 0228 · …to bring vigour to bear: making the stretching-forth — S. ἐπέκτασις: cf. Phil 3:13 (τοῖς δὲ ἔμπροσθεν ἐπεκτεινόμενος), the "stretching-forth" of Gregory of Nyssa's ascent.
  • 0229 · …through the heavens from knowledge to knowledge scientifically, — S. 2 Cor 3:18 ("from glory to glory"); Heb 4:14 ("passed through the heavens"). The adverbs πρακτικῶς / ἐπιστημονικῶς set the practical and the contemplative ascent side by side.
  • 0229 · …forerunner on our behalf in what is ours. — S. Heb 6:19–20. τὸ καθ᾿ ἡμᾶς, "in what is ours": the Latin expands nostra carne ac natura.
  • 0229 · …things that remove from God, and, without relation, — T. The bracketed infinitives stand in brackets in the text as given, apparently an editorial supplement; the Latin has only ex toto corde colere. A stray ει after them is logged.
  • 0229 · …put off the sin that so easily besets. — S. Heb 12:1 (τὴν εὐπερίστατον ἁμαρτίαν).
  • 0229 · …me search out the discourse against the Diaetetes — P. The Diaetetes (Διαιτητής, "Arbiter") of John Philoponus, the tritheist-leaning Miaphysite treatise; Maximus puns on it as Diaeretes (Διαιρέτης), "Divider."
  • 0229 · …world, who bears the name of the resurrection — P. Anastasius I, patriarch of Antioch (559–570, 593–599): Theopolis is Antioch's sixth-century name, and Ἀναστάσιος "bears the name" of ἀνάστασις, resurrection. His discourse against the Arbiter survives only in fragments such as these.
  • 0229 · …from them—I mean the work and the action — T. πρᾶξις, paired with ἔργον as the product of operation, is rendered "action" in this piece (the ascetic "practice" of the glossary is a different sense; Latin actio).
  • 0232 · …work and action, he goes through it thus: — S. Anastasius of Antioch, Against the Arbiter (lost; these three citations are known chiefly from Maximus).
  • 0232 · …but to call their property one, without confusion — T. The clause is elliptical in the Greek and in the Latin (unamque earum citra confusionem dicere proprietatem). The sense Maximus draws from it in the next sentence is that one property cannot be said without confusion; the words are rendered as printed.
  • 0233 · …of the natures that have essentially run together, — T. Migne prints τά τε θάτερα φύσεων (confirmed on the plate), which construes poorly (θάτερα with a plural genitive); the Latin reads utraque earum … naturarum. The English "the things of either" follows the Latin, a conjecture.
  • 0233 · …Gregory, the theologian and great herald of the — T. The bracketed ἀληθείας is printed in brackets; the Latin reads veritatis praeconis.
  • 0233 · …not even opposed to God, being wholly deified», — S. Gregory Nazianzen, Or. 30.12 (Fourth Theological Oration), on John 6:38: τὸ γὰρ ἐκείνου θέλειν οὐδὲν ὑπεναντίον Θεῷ, θεωθὲν ὅλον.
  • 0233 · …which and out of which the industrious man — T. τῷ φιλοπόνῳ: the unnamed author of the interpretation Marinus had sent; not John Philoponus (Latin viro industrio).
  • 0233 · …and natural will of the ensouled flesh assumed — T. Read προσληφθείσης "assumed" with the Latin assumpta; the text has προληφθείσης "taken beforehand." Logged as a doubt.
  • 0233 · …says: «For his willing is wise and circumspect», — S. Not in the received text of Or. 30.12; apparently the reading or paraphrase the interpreter cited. Cf. Or. 30.12.
  • 0233 · …deified with paroxytone accent, as from certain copies, — T. Read paroxytone, the word is θεόθεν, "from God" (θεόθεν ὅλον, "wholly from God"), as the Latin prints it (a Deo totum*); oxytone, θεωθέν, "deified." Maximus defends the oxytone.
  • 0233 · …one will be brought in by the opponents, — T. Migne prints δέοι (confirmed on the plate), which does not construe here; the English reads δέει, "for fear", a conjecture.
  • 0233 · …were hinting at a relational and hypostatic union, — T. "Hypostatic" here means a union of persons by relation, the Nestorian reading; the Latin renders personalem.
  • 0236 · …«resisting for the most part and wrestling against»? — S. Gregory Nazianzen, Or. 30.12: τοῦ καθ᾿ ἡμᾶς οὐ πάντως ἑπομένου τῷ θείῳ, ἀλλ᾿ ἀντιπίπτοντος ὡς τὰ πολλὰ καὶ ἀντιπαλαίοντος.
  • 0237 · …along with them in these is not lawful. — T. Read οὐ μὴν θέμις, "yet it is not lawful"; the text prints οὐ μὴ θέμις, which does not construe. The Latin agrees in sense (id vero nefas est).
  • 0237 · …as to the head of the whole body, — S. Cf. Eph 4:15–16; Col 1:18.
  • 0237 · …as the sun the vapour of the earth, — S. Cf. Ps 67:3 LXX (ὡς τήκεται κηρὸς ἀπὸ προσώπου πυρός).
  • 0237 · …Honorius, the pope of the of the Romans, — P. Honorius I, pope 625–638, whose letter to Sergius, patriarch of Constantinople (610–638), spoke of "one will" in Christ and was later condemned at the Sixth Council (681). Maximus here defends it.

Chunk 0026 (mo-026)
